## Document 157: AIがサイバー攻撃を自動化する時代、サイバー株は上がるのか ～攻撃能力の急伸と企業業績・株価から検証する～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AIがサイバー攻撃を自動化する時代、サイバー株は上がるのか ～攻撃能力の急伸と企業業績・株価から検証する～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年 10 月 6 日

1/6 
AI がサイバー攻撃を自動化する時代、サイバー株
は上がるのか 
～攻撃能力の急伸と企業業績・株価から検証する～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  柏村 祐

（要旨） 
○ AI によるサイバー攻撃の自動化・高度化が現実のものとなっている。豪政府システムへのAI
侵入事件などが示すように、AI は与えられた目的を達成する過程で、許可された範囲を超え
てシステムにアクセスしうることが明らかになった。国内でも 9 月下旬以降、幅広い業種の
企業で不正アクセスの公表が相次いでいる。 
○ AI エージェントのサイバー課題解決能力はここ数年で急速に向上しており、サイバーセキュ
リティ企業の売上高・利益も足元で好調に推移している。 
○ 一方で、高度な AI モデルの登場は既存のサイバーセキュリティ製品の陳腐化リスクへの懸
念を生み、関連株が下落するというパラドックスも起きている。「サイバー株なら何でも上
がる」という安易な連想買いには注意が必要である。 
○ 個人投資家は、サイバーセキュリティセクター全体を丸ごと買うのではなく、「AIネイティ
ブ」なサイバーセキュリティ企業や、AIを活用した防衛技術を持つ巨大テック企業（メガテ
ック）への選別投資が求められる。

1. 豪政府システムへの「AI侵入事件」が鳴らす警鐘 
2026 年9月、オーストラリアのアルバニージー首相は、同国の公的医療保険制度（Medicare）
の統計ポータルサイトに、OpenAIのAIエージェントが無断で侵入していたと発表した。注目すべ
きは、これが悪意あるハッカーによるサイバー攻撃ではなく、OpenAIの研究チームがAIに調査を
実行させる過程で起きた点だ。AIは調査中にアクセス制限に直面すると、自ら制限を回避する方
法を見つけ出し、非公開のファイルにまでアクセスしたという。この事例は、AIが与えられた目
的を達成する過程で、許可された範囲を超えてシステムにアクセスしうることを示した。 
サイバー攻撃の脅威は、海の向こうだけの話ではない。日本でも9月下旬以降、物流、交通、
小売、カーシェアリングなど幅広い業種の企業が、不正アクセスによる個人情報流出の可能性を
相次いで公表している。これらの攻撃にAIが用いられたかどうかは明らかになっていないが、サ
イバー攻撃の影響が業種を問わず及んでいることは確かだ。政府も10月1 日から、能動的サイバ

### Page 2

2/6 
ー防御の一環として、重大なサイバー攻撃を未然に防ぐため、警察や自衛隊が攻撃元のサーバー
等にアクセスし、無害化措置を講じることを可能にした。 
AIがサイバー攻撃を自動化・高度化させる時代が到来しつつある。企業や政府の防御需要は急
増し、サイバーセキュリティ企業には特需がもたらされると期待される。個人投資家の間の「AI
の次なる恩恵銘柄はサイバー株」という見方にも、一定の説得力がある。しかし、 「サイバー株な
ら何でも上がる」と考えるのは早計である。AIによる攻撃の自動化・高度化は、攻撃件数を増や
すだけでなく、従来のセキュリティ製品や防御手法の有効性そのものを揺るがしかねないから
だ。本稿では、AIの攻撃能力の現在地と、それがサイバーセキュリティ企業の業績および株価に
与える影響を、データに基づいて検証する。そのうえで、 「サイバー株＝安全確実な成長株」とい
う見方をどう見直すべきか、個人投資家に向けて投資戦略を考えるための新たな視点を提示した
い。

2. AIの攻撃能力急伸とサイバーセキュリティ企業の業績・株価への影響 
(1) AIは人間なしでどこまで攻撃できるのか 
AIエージェントのサイバー攻撃関連能力は急速に高まっている。スタンフォード大学人間中心
AI研究所（Stanford HAI）が公表した「AI Index Report 2026」によれば、AIは人間が逐次指示
を与えなくても、専門的なサイバー課題を自律的に解ける水準に達しつつある。 
同レポートで紹介されている「Cybench」は、暗号、Webセキュリティ、リバースエンジニアリ
ング、フォレンジック、エクスプロイトなど、40の専門レベルのサイバーセキュリティ課題を評
価するベンチマークである。資料1 は、人間のガイドなし（unguided）でAIが課題を解決できた
割合の推移を示している。これを見ると、2024年時点ではわずか15％であった解決率が、2025年
には 82％へと急伸し、2026年には93％に達している。2年間で 78ポイントの上昇であり、AIが
自律的にサイバー課題を解く能力が大きく向上していることがわかる。

### Page 3

3/6 
資料 1 AI エージェントの自律的なサイバー課題解決能力は急伸している 
（注）サイバーセキュリティのベンチマーク「Cybench」における、人間のガイドなし（unguided）での課題解決率（％）の推移。
Cybench は暗号、Web セキュリティ、リバースエンジニアリング、フォレンジック、エクスプロイトなど 40 の専門レベル課題を評価
するもの。 
（出所）Stanford HAI, “AI Index Report 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(2) サイバー需要は企業の売上・利益に届いているのか 
こうしたAIによるサイバー攻撃の脅威が高まるなか、防御側であるサイバーセキュリティ企業
への需要拡大が期待される。実際、その需要はすでに企業業績に表れている。 
Nasdaqが公表した「Q1 2026 HXRXL Cybersecurity Commentary」によれば、サイバーセキュリ
ティ関連銘柄で構成される「Nasdaq ISE Cyber Security Select Index（HXRXL） 」構成企業の
2026 年第1 四半期決算は極めて好調であった。同レポートによると、構成企業の売上高は前年同
期比で 11％増加し、純利益は同29.3％の増加を記録しており、売上高以上に純利益が大きく伸び
ていることが確認できる。さらに、市場予想に対する結果をまとめた資料2を見ても、売上高で
予想を上回った企業は指数ウェイトの92.2％を占め、純利益に至っては100％の企業が予想を上
回った。サイバーセキュリティ需要の強さは、売上高と利益の双方に表れている。

### Page 4

4/6 
資料 2 サイバーセキュリティ企業の業績は市場予想を大きく上回る 
（注）Nasdaq ISE Cyber Security Select Index（HXRXL）構成企業の 2026 年第 1 四半期決算の集計結果。市場予想に対する売上
高および純利益の予想超え・予想未達の企業数（指数ウェイトベース）、ならびに平均上振れ・下振れ率（％）を示す。 
（出所）Nasdaq, “Q1 2026 HXRXL Cybersecurity Commentary”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(3) それでもサイバー株は上がるのか 
業績が好調であれば株価も上昇すると考えるのが自然だが、 「AIによる攻撃高度化＝サイバー株
上昇」とは必ずしも言い切れない。AIは防御需要を増やす一方で、既存のサイバーセキュリティ
製品を陳腐化させるとの懸念も生んでいるからだ。 
国際決済銀行（BIS）が2026 年7月に公表したレポート「A Mythos moment? Frontier AI and 
cyber risk」は、このパラドックスを指摘している。2026年 4月にAnthropicが高度なサイバー攻
撃能力を持つAIモデル「Mythos」を発表した際、サイバーセキュリティ関連株は大きく下落し
た。資料3 は、Mythos発表前を0とした場合のサイバーセキュリティ関連株の累積異常リターン
（CAR）を示している。発表後、サイバーセキュリティETF（IHAK）や主要企業（CRWD、PANWな
ど）のCARは軒並みマイナスに転じ、一部の系列ではグラフ上で約−30％の大幅なマイナスを記録
した。BISは、この市場反応について「新しいAIモデルの登場により、既存のサイバーセキュリテ
ィ製品やサービスが陳腐化する（obsolescence）という投資家の懸念を反映している」との解釈
を紹介している。つまり、AIの進化はサイバーセキュリティ市場全体を拡大させる可能性が高い
ものの、同時にゲームチェンジを引き起こし、旧来の技術やサービスに依存する企業の競争力を
急速に奪うリスクを孕んでいる。

### Page 5

5/6 
資料 3 高度な AI モデルの発表後、サイバーセキュリティ関連株は下落した 
（注）2026 年 4 月の高度な AI モデル「Mythos」発表前後の、サイバーセキュリティ関連株の累積異常リターン（CAR、％）。発表前
を 0 として推移を示している。「Glasswing partners」は CrowdStrike（CRWD）および Palo Alto Networks（PANW）を指す。 
（出所）Bank for International Settlements, “BIS Bulletin No. 129: A Mythos moment? Frontier AI and cyber risk”をもとに第一ラ
イフ資産運用経済研究所作成

3. AI時代のサイバー株投資～「陳腐化リスク」を回避する新戦略～ 
第 2 章で見てきたように、AI はサイバー攻撃能力を飛躍的に高めている。また、足元のサイバ
ーセキュリティ企業の業績も好調である。しかし、最先端のAIモデルが登場した際には、市場は
既存のサイバーセキュリティ製品の「陳腐化リスク」に敏感に反応し、関連株は大きく売り込ま
れた。ここからいえるのは、サイバーセキュリティ市場全体は今後も拡大が見込まれるものの、
企業間の「勝ち負け」はこれまで以上に鮮明になるということだ。こうした環境変化のなか、個
人投資家には従来の投資アプローチを根本から見直すことが求められる。 
第一に、サイバーセキュリティセクター全体を丸ごと買うアプローチ（テーマ型ETFやインデ
ックスファンドなど）には注意が必要である。市場全体が成長しても、AIの進化に取り残され、
陳腐化して淘汰される旧世代の企業をポートフォリオに抱え込むリスクが高まるからだ。足元の
業績や過去の市場シェアだけで安心することはできない。第二に、個別銘柄を選別する際は、 「AI
ネイティブ」なサイバーセキュリティ企業であるかどうかが最大の焦点となる。攻撃側がAI を駆
使する以上、防御側もAIをシステムの中心に据え、未知の脅威を自律的に検知・遮断できるプラ
ットフォームを持っていなければ対抗できない。レガシーな製品を販売している企業ではなく、
クラウドベースで膨大なデータをAI で解析し、防御機能を継続的に高度化できる企業を見極める
必要がある。第三に、サイバー防衛の投資対象を「セキュリティ専業企業」以外にも広げること
である。 
最先端のAI モデルを開発し、強固なクラウドインフラを持つ巨大テック企業（メガテック）
は、自社のAI を活用した防衛技術を、顧客向けの強力なセキュリティサービスとして提供し始め

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。

ている。豊富な資金力とAI 開発力を持つメガテックを、サイバー防衛の担い手として評価し、ポ
ートフォリオの中核に据えることも、AI時代の投資戦略として有効な選択肢となるだろう。

## Document 176: ASML零部件价格全线上调10%，三星SK海力士接受涨价，半导体设备成本压力蔓延

Source PDF: all report pdf/ASML零部件价格全线上调10%，三星SK海力士接受涨价，半导体设备成本压力蔓延.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-10
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

ASML 罕⻅对三星、 SK 海⼒⼠全线零部件涨价 10% 。受 AI 扩产潮驱动，设备供需严重失
衡，应⽤材料等巨头纷纷跟进。晶圆⼚议价优势不再。
摘要：
全球最⼤半导体设备制造商 ASML 宣布对韩国客⼾实施零部件价格全线上调 10% ，三星电⼦与
SK 海⼒⼠已接受这⼀⽅案，涨价将于明年 1 ⽉起正式⽣效。这标志着半导体设备供应链的定价权
正加速向供应商⼀侧倾斜，⾏业成本压⼒持续扩散。
据韩国科技媒体 The Lec 报道， ASML 总部于上⽉初通过韩国法⼈向三星和 SK 海⼒⼠发出涨价通
知，经双⽅采购部⻔协商后确认。此次涨价覆盖 EUV 与 DUV 光刻机的全部零部件，包括定期采
购的消耗性部件，以及因故障或性能下降⽽需更换的核⼼组件，涉及光学系统、光源、精密驱
动装置等。
此次全线⼀次性上调 10% 被业界视为异常举措。 ASML 此前通常仅在原材料价格⼤幅上涨或特定
材料采购困难时对个别零部件进⾏单独调价。与此同时， ASML 正与主要客⼾就新设备价格上调
展开谈判，设备本体涨价预期也在升温。业界普遍认为，三星和 SK 海⼒⼠接受涨价后，其他设

备与零部件供应商的提价要求将获得更⼤⽀撑。
ASML 零部件涨价：覆盖范围⼴，时机罕⻅
此次涨价范围涵盖 ASML 供应韩国市场的所有替换⽤设备零部件， EUV 与 DUV 光刻机均在其列。
从定期更换的消耗品到关键结构件，价格将于 2026 年 1 ⽉起统⼀上调 10% 。
ASML 将安装于客⼾⼯⼚的设备服务、零部件及升级业务单独核算。 2024 年该业务收⼊达 81.93
亿欧元，占公司总营收 326.67 亿欧元的约 25% 。 ASML 预计今年这部分收⼊将增⻓逾 30% 。
⼀位半导体⾏业⼈⼠表⽰，零部件构成要素的整体成本确实有所上升，但 ASML 此举也有在客⼾
盈利改善之际提升⾃⾝利润率的考量。
设备本体涨价谈判同步推进， TSMC 态度存分歧
在零部件之外，新订单设备的价格上调同样在酝酿之中。 ASML ⽬前正与主要客⼾就设备价格上
调进⾏磋商。
据 The Information 此前 7 ⽉报道， ASML 已通知中国半导体企业等客⼾， DUV 设备价格将上调
10% ，部分客⼾已接受。然⽽，作为 ASML 最⼤客⼾的台积电据悉对设备涨价持反对⽴场。

三星和 SK 海⼒⼠率先接受零部件涨价，被业界解读为⼀个重要信号 —— 在供需格局已然逆转的
背景下，⼤型客⼾的议价空间正在收窄。
涨价潮蔓延，多家设备巨头跟进
ASML 并⾮孤例。半导体设备领域的涨价趋势已在多家头部企业中蔓延。
美国应⽤材料公司在今年 8 ⽉的业绩发布中表⽰，新产品与现有产品价格均已提升。⽇本东京电
⼦在 7 ⽉末的业绩说明会上表⽰，正推进包括在售设备在内的价格调整，以应对成本上升压⼒。
美国等离⼦电源设备商 Advanced Energy 也宣布对部分产品线提价。

写评论
图⽚
供需失衡是核⼼驱动⼒
此轮涨价潮的根本原因在于供需结构性失衡。 AI 数据中⼼投资热潮带动存储与晶圆代⼯⼚商同步
扩产，设备与零部件订单集中涌⼊，⽽供应商扩充产能需要时间。与此同时，通胀与原材料价
格上涨也推⾼了供应商的成本负担。
这与过去半导体⼚商凭借采购规模优势压低供应商单价的格局形成鲜明对⽐。当前，供应商的
价格谈判能⼒已显著增强。
⼀位⾏业⼈⼠指出，⼤规模扩产正在实际落地，设备与零部件已进⼊供应短缺阶段，随着晶圆
投⼊量持续增加，耗材领域也将出现⼤规模供应缺⼝。

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The Cotality home value index, covering all of Australia,
fell 1.1% in September with downward revisions to the
previous two months again shaving a further 0.4% off prices.
The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2% from their

March peak. Annual growth is flat.
The price correction remains broad, Cotality reporting 97% of
capital city suburbs experienced falls over the three months
to September, Darwin the only city to record gains. Sydney
and Melbourne continue to see more pronounced declines,
albeit moderating a touch, particularly in Melbourne.
Against this, declines in Brisbane, Adelaide and Perth have
accelerated over the last few months. Across segments, unit
prices and lower price tiers continue to track slightly milder

falls than houses and top tiers.
Seasonal variations accentuated price weakness through winter
but are now neutral. The peak-to-trough price decline nationally
is slightly milder at 4.6% in seasonally adjusted terms. Unless
specified, figures in titles, tables and the commentary below are
in non-seasonally adjusted terms, consistent with the ‘headline’
figures reported by Cotality. However, figures shown in charts
are in seasonally adjusted terms.

Preliminary estimates of turnover show rolling 3mth volumes
down 3.9%qtr in September and 17.4%yr with previous
estimates revised 1-2ppts lower. The monthly pattern
suggests small gains in July and August have given way to
another sizeable fall in September.
Looking at the price performance across different jurisdictions:
• Sydney prices dropped 1.4% fall in September, monthly
declines averaging 1.6% over the previous four months,
tracking a 17% annualised pace of decline since May. All

segments and tiers recorded falls in Q3 but with top and
middle tier houses notably weaker.
• Melbourne prices fell 0.7%mth, a slower pace than other
capitals. This likely reflects the weaker starting point coming
into the correction, prices already back to the levels seen in
mid-2021. As elsewhere, 'top tier' houses prices are leading
the decline. 'Low tier' unit prices mostly held their ground in
Q3, prices in this sub-segment already plumbing 10yr lows.

• Brisbane was the weakest capital city price-wise, recording
a 1.5% drop in September with previous monthly declines
also revised down heavily (August from 1% to 1.6% and
July from 1.2% to 1.7%). The combined effect has taken
the rolling 3mth change to –4.7%qtr from –2.7%qtr last
month and –0.6%qtr in July. At 5.9%yr, annual growth is
still positive but losing altitude quickly, having run at 18%yr
as recently as May. Sellers have been slow to respond to

Losing altitude

the sudden stop with total listings up over 50% vs a year

• Adelaide dwelling prices fell 1.3%mth, the August decline

resilient in Hobart (–0.5%mth, 7%yr) but weaker in

milder declines in NSW (–0.7%) and Victoria (0.3%). Annual
prices growth is in the 3.4-6.3%yr range.

Our recently released Housing Pulse report (here) provides
a deeper diver into the situation in Australia's housing
markets. While the absence of selling pressure, still tight
supply-demand balance and sentiment mix suggests the
correction was still relatively 'orderly' through the September
quarter, the latest monthly data shows momentum remains
unambiguously negative.
That said, there are clearly more headwinds to come. The
RBA latest interest rate rise and what now looks to be a

more likely prospect of a follow-up increase at its meeting in
early November will impact in coming months. Next week's
consumer sentiment update will show how the change, and
the latest round of weak updates, is shaping housing-related
sentiment. Either way, momentum looks set to remain weak
into year-end and, barring a significantly improved inflation
and interest rate outlook, early 2027.

## Document 207: Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-10)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ mulling the minutes (2026-10-10).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-10
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
11:10 October 09 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
The only data release of note in Australia this week was the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment
Survey, which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At 80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to
deeply pessimistic levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock when readings were among

the weakest in the survey’s more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest rate pressures are
once again clearly front of mind for consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a year ago’

### Page 2

and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0% and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the
RBA’s September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact – sentiment tanked 20% between
those surveyed before the RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.

Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient, but they still weakened, with the one-year
and five-year ahead sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers are becoming more
‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively low
versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip
demand. We expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force Survey – see our preview
for more detail. All told, with higher interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening jobs
market all weighing at once, households are entering the final months of the year on the back foot.

Offshore, the focus was on US data.

Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that September non-farm payrolls came in
softer than expected across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k expected), and there
were sizeable downward revisions to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July and August
payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure &
hospitality and education & health services industries, although construction and manufacturing
employment continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related investment. The unemployment rate
edged up to 4.2%, with softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force participation to
61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average pace of job creation slowing to around 50k.
However, softer payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market deterioration. Lower
immigration and ageing demographics have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more consistent with a labour market that is
gradually normalising than one entering a meaningful downturn.

The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish message, with participants noting they
had "not seen sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and expressing concern that
inflation risks remained "skewed to the upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing persistent goods price strength to the
effects of the AI buildout despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was also concern
that, after more than five years of inflation above target, elevated inflation could begin to influence
inflation expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In contrast, labour market conditions
were generally viewed as stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority noted the
labour market had strengthened somewhat recently, several participants highlighted unusually low
dynamism, characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding rate and elevated long-term
unemployment. Importantly, participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth was moderate
and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2 percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor
market was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".

### Page 3

Related articles
A key focus was the interaction between AI investment, productive capacity and inflation. While
participants broadly expected AI-related investment to boost productivity growth and potential output
over time, several warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand to outpace aggregate
supply over the medium term, putting upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some
participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate and several judged the current policy
stance to be "not restrictive or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants supported
the September rate increase, and most assessed that another increase in the federal funds rate would
likely be appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain data dependent.

The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted a similar picture of an economy that
remains resilient but is gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9 from 55.4 in
August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export
orders, which slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight months. Labour market
conditions improved modestly with the employment index moving to broadly neutral territory 50.1 after
two months below 50, supported by elevated backlogs and still-solid demand. Respondent
commentary illustrated the mixed nature of labour demand, with some firms reporting they were "filling
positions vacated due to promotions or retirements", while others cited "restructuring due to efficiencies
gained using AI tools". Cost pressures also remained elevated, with the prices paid index rising to 74.0,
its highest level since July 2022 and around 18pts above its historical average, while fuel costs, tariffs
and supply chain constraints remained the most frequently cited concerns. Overall, the report suggests
services activity remains consistent with ongoing expansion, albeit at a slower pace, while labour
demand and pricing pressures remain firm.

Overall, the data supports our view that the FOMC will move to a broadly neutral policy stance by the
end of the year with one more rate hike, although the case for a back-to-back move in October has
become less clear cut.

## Document 229: El Niño 2026–2027_ multiple pressures on prices and supplies (2026-09-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/El Niño 2026–2027_ multiple pressures on prices and supplies (2026-09-30).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

30 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EL NIÑO 2026–2027: MULTIPLE PRESSURES ON PRICES AND SUPPLIES
Hélène Drouot
The 2026–2027 El Niño climate event is expected to be one of the most intense in the last fifty years, with sea-surface-temperature
anomalies potentially exceeding +3°C in the equatorial Pacific. According to the World Meteorological Organisation (WMO), El Niño
is expected to persist until February 2027, with asymmetrical effects, as South-East Asia and northern Latin America will experience

droughts, while southern Brazil and Argentina could see heavy rainfall. Above all, El Niño is compounding the effects of global warming
and exacerbating existing extreme-weather events (droughts, floods and heatwaves). The economic effects of this climate shock mainly
play out through three avenues: agricultural commodity prices, energy and logistics (see heatmap).
Agricultural commodity prices: volatility is causing tensions, with inflationary effects

In India and South-East Asia, droughts will affect harvests, pushing up prices (particularly for coffee, cocoa, rice and palm oil). Conversely, in
southern Brazil and Argentina, heavy rainfall will boost soya and maize production, putting downward pressure on prices. According to the IMF,
El Niño causes global food prices to rise by between 5% (in a typical episode) and 8% (very high intensity) year-on-year, with the full impact being
felt 6 to 12 months after the climatic peak.

Therefore, for a given country, the weighting of food in its inflation index (CPI) plays a decisive role. As a result, all other things being equal, an 8%
rise in prices adds around 1 pp to inflation when food accounts for 13% of the CPI basket (average for countries shown in green on the heatmap),
1.8 pp when this share reaches 22% (yellow), 2.8 pp at 35% (orange) and nearly 4 pp above 49% (red).
The risks are skewed to the upside, as the 8% estimate does not take into account additional losses linked to droughts, floods and other extre -

me events, while policy responses to shocks (stockpiling by importers and increased export restrictions) may amplify and prolong price pres -
sures. The rice market between 2023 and 2024 illustrates this well, as the combination of rice-export restrictions imposed by India in July 2023
(in order to protect its domestic market) and of the drought in South-East Asia contributed to a 30% average increase in the price of rice over the
course of 2023 and kept global prices high for several months after the end of the El Niño episode.

Energy production sector: heavy reliance on hydroelectricity leaves the sector vulnerable to a second supply shock
Droughts will reduce hydroelectric power generation in Latin America and South-East Asia. As a result, some countries will have to turn to other,
more expensive solutions, such as fossil fuels, against a backdrop of already high oil and gas prices. This shift will increase global demand for
oil and put pressure on prices which, according to the IMF, could persist for up to four quarters after the initial El Niño shock. For example,

in Colombia, where hydropower accounts for 55% of electricity generation, a 30% drop in output (already seen in 2023–2024) could increase the
cost of electricity generation by 15 to 20% (assuming that the cost of thermal power generation is twice as high as hydropower). In Ecuador (where
hydropower accounts for 70% of electricity generation) and Vietnam (30%), electricity shortages are likely to disrupt local industries.
SOURCE: IMF, WORLD BANK, EMBER, BNP PARIBAS CALCULATIONS

Vulnerabilities differ, depending on a country’s dependence on food, energy and logistics

Reading note: Scores are standardised on a scale of 0 to 10 for ease of comparison between countries and supply channels.
- Food vulnerability: based on the weight of food in the CPI.
- Energy vulnerability: based on the share of hydropower in electricity generation.
- Logistics vulnerability: the ‘Trade and transport-related infrastructure’ component of the World Bank’s Logistics Perfor-

mance Index, which measures the quality of the infrastructure required for transport and international trade.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 30 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
Logistics: transport infrastructure is another point of vulnerability
The final channel relates to disruptions to freight transport against a backdrop of already weakened value chains. The variable used in our
heatmap is a proxy for economies’ abilities to absorb a shock (particularly a climate-related one) affecting their transport infrastructure (for

example, by maintaining traffic flow, diverting or reorganising transport flows). The heatmap highlights the particularly high vulnerability
of Latin America. Its capacity to absorb a logistics shock is relatively limited, and there is significant exposure at a strategic chokepoint, the
Panama Canal, through which approximately 5% of global trade passes. Low rainfall in the canal basin has already led to the number of
daily transits being reduced in September 2026 (as in 2023–2024); further restrictions are expected in October (to 29 transits per day, down

from 36 in August), which will increase transport times and costs. Europe is also being affected, with low water levels on the Rhine and the
Danube already disrupting river transport and electricity generation.
Latin America and South-East Asia are hit hardest
Therefore, the economies most at risk are not necessarily those that will suffer the greatest climate shock, but those where multiple trans -
mission channels converge, including a high weighting of food prices in inflation, reliance on hydroelectricity, fragile transport infrastructure

and limited fiscal room for manoeuvre. Latin America and South-East Asia are home to some of the most vulnerable countries, particularly
due to their dependence on food prices and hydroelectricity. Colombia and Ecuador are noteworthy for their high exposure to energy risk,
while Ghana, India and the Philippines are some of the economies most vulnerable to a food shock. Latin America faces a combination of
vulnerabilities, such as high exposure to food and energy price shocks in some countries, which are compounded by logistics risks, as currently

illustrated by the restrictions on traffic through the Panama Canal.

## Document 268: GLP-1能“减龄”2-4岁？

Source PDF: all report pdf/GLP-1能“减龄”2-4岁？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

诺和诺德与礼来同期公布 GLP-1 ⽣物学年龄数据，显⽰⽤药者的部分年龄时钟读数下降约 2
⾄ 4 年，但尚不能证明药物能够延缓健康⼈的衰⽼。⾯对专利到期与价格下⾏压⼒，两⼤巨
头正试图将估值锚从减重转向健康寿命，⽽真正的分⽔岭仍是随机对照试验。
摘要：
有意思的事情发⽣了。
10 ⽉ 1 ⽇，我们的国庆假期第⼀天，美国波⼠顿 ARDD （衰⽼研究与药物发现）会议的议程上，
出现了少⻅的⼀幕：诺和与礼来， GLP-1 的两⼤卖家，在同⼀周交出了同⼀类数据，⽣物学年龄
时钟。
数据分两份，⼀厚⼀薄。
厚的那份来⾃诺和诺德。汇总五项司美格鲁肽试验、 10,052 名参与者的蛋⽩组数据，约⼀半⽤
药、⼀半安慰剂，⾸次随访时 “ ⼼脏⽣物学年龄 ” 降低约 2 到 4 年。再⽤  SELECT 试验的效应数
据，在英国 19,117 ⼈的真实世界队列⾥建模，推算出 1.9 个额外⽣命年。
薄的那份来⾃礼来。 SURMOUNT-5 替尔泊肽臂的 71 对⾎样， 15 个表观遗传时钟读数，全部低于
实际流逝的 1.38 年，其中 2 个统计显著，中位差值约 1.1 年和 0.7 年。

两份数据讲述的是同⼀件故事：⽤了这个药的⼈，⽣物学年龄往回退了两三年。
与此同时， UCLA 表观时钟的发明⼈ Steve Horvath 给了⼀句分量很重的背书：两年是 “ 很强的效
应 ” ，这些数据可能证明  GLP-1 是  geroprotector ，也就是衰⽼保护剂。参与诺和测量的哈佛教授
Vadim Gladyshev ，措辞则冷静的多： “ 在不健康⼈群中，我确实认为它是抗衰⽼药；但在健康
⼈群中，没⼈知道。 ”
那句 “ 没⼈知道 ” ，可能才是这轮最新叙事⾥最诚实的⼀句。
⼀场被设计好的讨论
先说数字是怎么造出来的。
两家⽤的不是同⼀种钟。诺和诺德⽤的是蛋⽩组时钟，从⾎浆⾥⼏千种蛋⽩的浓度，反推各个
器官的 “ 年龄 ” ；礼来⽤的是表观遗传时钟，读 DNA 上的甲基化标记，看它们累积到哪个年份的⽔
平。前者更贴近当下的功能状态，后者更接近细胞的 “ 磨损计数 ” 。
诺和的报告⾥，整体⽣物学年龄差约 2 到 3 年，⼼脏那⼀个器官最多差 4 年。同⼀个分⼦，⼼脏的

时钟可以往回拨 4 年，其他组织⾛得更慢、更弱。衰⽼本来就没有统⼀的那块表，摆在台⾯上
的，恰好是⾛得最慢的⼏块。
礼来这边的边界划得更清楚。 71 对⾎样只有替尔泊肽⼀个臂，没有安慰剂、没有同期对照，公
司⾃⼰把它定义为 “ 初步、假设⽣成 ” 。 Kevin Duffin 在台上说得很直⽩：各个时钟⼤体讲的是同
⼀个故事，治疗后年龄读数下降， “ 但这绝不是所谓逆转 30 年 ” 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

图：礼来  SURMOUNT-5 替尔泊肽臂表观遗传⼦研究， 71 对基线⾄第 72 周配对⾎样。
最能说明问题的⼀处，是礼来⾃⼰的幻灯⽚。算出来的甲基化模型，成功预测了体重指数、腰
臀⽐、 HbA1c 、⾎糖和⾎脂向好的⽅向，却把  SHBG 、 TNF 受体和 VCAM1 预测成了上升，与现
实相反。
诺和那边也有⼀个反直觉的细节。在减重和剂量快速爬升的阶段，脂肪组织的蛋⽩质签名会短
暂显得 “ 更⽼ ” ，瘦素和 FABP4 等指标先变差，之后才转为中性或改善。就是说，所谓器官年龄，
并不是⼀个单向、单调的数字。
于是就能读出这场披露的意图。在 ARDD 这样的场合同步释放，先把故事讲圆，再等随机对照来
背书。
⽣意本该如此，但分⼨必须钉死：这组数据被设计成 “ 引发讨论 ” ，没有⼀项被设计成 “ ⽀持标
签 ” 。 Horvath 在现场最直接的提醒也是这⼀句：下⼀步需要的，是真正的对照臂。
估值锚搬家：从体重秤到健康寿命
为什么两家巨头挑这个时点讲故事？答案得往资产负债表上找。

GLP-1 的估值锚，⻓期钉在 “ 减重市场规模 ” 上。⽽这是⼀个正在被两⾯夹击的品类。
⼀⾯是专利。司美格鲁肽在中国的核⼼化合物专利已于 2026 年 3 ⽉ 20 ⽇到期，美国、⽇本、欧洲
要等到 2031 到 2032 年；替尔泊肽的专利在中美都到 2036 年，外围专利还能延到 2039 ⾄ 2041
年。
中国到期这⼀下，年内就有超过 10 家本⼟药企排队递交仿制药和⽣物类似药申请，⻬鲁、⽯
药、成都倍特、华东医药、联邦制药等都在列，化学改良型仿制药预计 2026 年三季度起陆续获
批。
另⼀⾯是价格。诺和诺德已宣布 2027 年全线降价 35% 到 50% ；礼来给  Zepbound 做梯度定价，
每⽉ 299 到 449 美元，⼝服的  Foundayo 起步价压到 149 美元。分析师们⼀度把 2030 年的全球减
重市场预期到 1500 亿甚⾄ 2000 亿美元，如今普遍下调到 950 亿⾄ 1050 亿。

### Page 3

图：两台时钟⽅向相反：⼀台往回拨，⼀台只会⾛到零
把⼀个品类的价值主张，从 “ 减重 ” 升维到 “ 健康寿命 ” ，就是在这样的背景下发⽣的。
逻辑链条并不复杂。⼀旦 “ 延缓衰⽼ ” 被接受为⼀种价值主张，整个适应症合集就能被打包进同⼀
张标签：慢性肾病、代谢相关脂肪性肝炎、⼼⾎管、阿尔茨海默、射⾎分数保留型⼼衰。定价
⽅式也随之切换，从 “ 每⼈每年治疗费 ” ，换成 “ 每⼈每年避免的并发症⽀出 ” 。后者⼤得多，也难
证得多。
铺垫早已开始。 Wegovy 已经获批⽤于降低符合条件的肥胖或超重⼼⾎管病患者的严重⼼⾎管事
件⻛险， Zepbound 已经获批治疗肥胖成⼈的中重度阻塞性睡眠呼吸暂停。诺和诺德在探索免疫
介导的炎症性疾病、疼痛与成瘾、⼥性与男性健康；公司 CEO 杜⻨克 2026 年 6 ⽉接受彭博采访
时说得更直接， GLP-1 这条产品线可以⾛进⻓寿研究与医美。
真正的分⽔岭，在德州
2026 年初，美国卫⽣⾼级研究计划局（ ARPA-H ）投⼊最⾼ 3800 万美元，在德州启动⼀项名为

VITAL-H 的试验，由  UT Health 圣安东尼奥的  Barshop ⻓寿与衰⽼研究所牵头。
它和波⼠顿这批数据的区别，只有两个词：随机，健康。
试验计划⼊组 726 名 60 到 65 岁的健康成年⼈，南德州作为招募核⼼，特意纳⼊在临床研究中⻓期
代表性不⾜的西班⽛裔社区。参与者被随机分配到三种已获批药物之⼀的组别或安慰剂组：雷
帕霉素、司美格鲁肽、达格列净。
它的终点，指向的是 “ 内在能⼒ ” ，也就是⾏动能⼒、认知、⼼理状态、感官功能的复合评分，⽤
可穿戴设备配合分散式评估来收集。研究者想做的，是把这个指标做成监管机构认可的、可⽤
于健康寿命药物的终点。
时间表⽐报道⾥的乐观说法要⻓。多家媒体称 “ 结果可能在⼀两年内出现 ” ，但按公开的项⽬安
排，⼊组预计 2027 年才开始。这是全⽂⾥最需要耐⼼的⼀处。
耐⼼⾥藏着此前所有数据的空缺。前后所有的 “ 年轻 2 到 4 年 ” ，都发⽣在不健康⼈群⾥：⼼⾎管
⾼危、肥胖、代谢异常。这些药物可能只是在修复疾病造成的损伤，⽽不是在延缓⼀个健康⼈

⾝上的衰⽼过程。Gladyshev 那句 “ 健康⼈群中没⼈知道 ” ，点破的正是这块空缺。
回到德州。如果这项试验读出阳性， GLP-1 将跃升为跨越⽀付⽅、跨越适应症、跨越卫⽣经济学
模型的新品类；如果阴性，它会退回原位，做⼀个优秀的代谢病药物。这会是未来数年⾥，代
谢赛道最重要的单⼀读出之⼀。

### Page 4

图：监管真正要解的，是⼀道 “ 终点阶梯 ” 的题
下⼀个竞争指标是肌⾁
叙事⼀旦转换，竞争的坐标跟变化。
过去在中国市场⽐的是 “ 减重百分⽐ ” ，⼀台体重秤就能算出来。下⼀阶段，指标会升级为 “ 体成
分加并发症终点加⽣活质量 ” 。 GLP-1 与 GIP 的组合、⼝服⼩分⼦、 amylin 、 activin 与 myostatin
⼏条路径的相对排序，都会被重新洗牌。
但肌⾁这条路，已经有过⼀次完整的教训。
诺华的  bimagrumab 是保肌赛道最被寄予期望的分⼦之⼀。在 70 岁以上符合肌少症标准的⼈群
⾥，⽤药组⼿臂和腿部的肌⾁量⽐安慰剂组多增， 7% 对 1% 。可肌⾁功能与⾏动能⼒的各项指
标，依然没能显著优于安慰剂。量增加了，⼈没有变得更有⼒⽓。这个从肌⾁量到⾝体功能的
落差，在整个肌⽣成抑制素类药物⾥反复出现。
2026 年的  BELIEVE ⼆期试验给出了同⼀个形状。 507 名肥胖或超重成⼈， bimagrumab 单药减
重约 9.3 公⽄，司美格鲁肽单药约 14.2 公⽄，两者联合约 17.8 公⽄。联合组 92.3% 的减重来⾃脂

肪，瘦⾁损失只有 1.1% 到 2.6% 。数字⾮常漂亮，但在握⼒和⾝体功能评分上，各组之间依然没
有显著差异。
另⼀条路径演⽰了代价。 Regeneron 的  COURAGE 试验⾥，司美格鲁肽单药减掉的体重中约三
分之⼀来⾃瘦⾁；加⼀款抗肌⽣成抑制素抗体，能把瘦⾁损失减少⼤约⼀半；再加⼀层抗 activin
抗体，能把脂肪占⽐推到 92.6% 。但停药的不良事件⽐例，从单药组的 4.6% 升到三联组的
28.3% ，三联组⾥出现了两例死亡。
把六项随机试验排在⼀起，有⼀个统计很难看：增加的瘦⾁量真正转化为功能改善，只发⽣过
⼀次。
结语
回到波⼠顿那间会议室。
⼀组被设计来引发讨论的数据，跑不过⼀组还在等⼊组的随机对照。 GLP-1 与 “ 健康寿命 ” 的距
离，终究在时间⾥。

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
TODAY’S INSIGHTS
Financial Markets
• Share markets generally stalled across the globe. In the US,
stocks partially retraced gains from earlier in the week, with
the S&P 500 (–0.2%), Dow Jones (–0.7%) and NASDAQ
(–0.2%) all declining. Stocks fared worse across the pond,

with the Euro Stoxx 50 effectively unwinding all gains in
recent days (–1.5%), marked by chunky declines in Germany’s
DAX (–1.4%) and France’s CAC (–1.2%), alongside a larger
decline in London’s FTSE 100 (–0.8%). Stocks were also
weaker across much of Asia, falling in Tokyo (–0.9%), Hong
Kong (–0.6%) and Seoul (–2.0%). The ASX 200 only edged
slightly lower, down –0.1%, but futures markets are pointing
to a weaker open this morning.
•  The treasury yield curve steepened overnight. Gains in the

shorter-end saw 2-year yields fall 3bps to 4.77% – this was
despite a strong lift in consumer inflation expectations and
a hawkish FOMC Minutes, with market pricing for further
rate hikes from the FOMC instead softening at the margin.
Meanwhile, elevated yields at the long end drew very strong
demand at the latest 10-year auction, stabilising 10-year
yields at around 5.28%.
•  Bonds were more varied elsewhere, with Bunds bull
steepening amid a steady long-end, while Gilts bear

steepened amid a 6bp lift in the 10-year. ACGBs were
broadly steady during local trading, with yields inching only
slightly higher, though that gave way to gains in futures
trading overnight. Swaps markets continue to have around
6-7bps priced in for the RBA’s November meeting, while a
rate hike in December is seen at closer to 50/50 odds.
•  The USD bounced back overnight as the DXY rallied to an
intraday high of 102.5 before settling 0.4% higher at around

102.3. The Euro led the way lower, falling 0.6% versus the
greenback to just under USD1.12, back around its recent
lows. The Sterling shared a similar fate, down 0.5% toward
the USD1.321 level, remaining largely rangebound between
USD1.32-1.33 since late September. The Yen bucked the
wider trend, holding broadly steady. The Aussie dollar
meanwhile fell 0.3% to around USD0.696, unwinding gains
from earlier in the week.
•  Crude oil prices remain elevated as markets try to evaluate

the net impact of opposing forces – signs of recovering flows
of oil out of the Middle East is a positive, but reports that
Iran has increased the number of attacks on ships transiting
the Strait of Hormuz has kept a floor under prices. Brent was
up 0.5% to US$101.1/bbl overnight, still unable to break
sustainably under the US$100/bbl mark as of yet. Copper
prices lifted 0.4% to US$14,476 on the LME, gains being
driven by risks of tighter supply amid strikes at a major

Chilean mine, though the turn in risk sentiment overnight and
firmer USD capped gains. Gold prices meanwhile fell 1.3% to
around US$4,111/oz.
International Data
In the US, the FOMC’s Minutes  from the September meeting
reinforced the hawkish sentiment present at the time of the
decision to raise the fed funds rate by 25bps to a midpoint
of 3.875%. “All” participants supported the decision to raise
rates in September, and “most” saw further tightening by

year-end as appropriate. Underscoring that view was the fact
that “almost all” participants saw inflation risks as tilted to the
upside. Importantly, the assessment of the inflation outlook at
the time had already anticipated a 0.2ppt downward revision
to PCE inflation from the BEA’s change in methodology, which
ended up being 0.3ppt in the event.
The NY Fed’s measure of consumer inflation expectations
for the year ahead meanwhile surprised to the upside, rising

from 3.58% in for to 3.90% in September, marking the highest
result so far this year. Expectations also nudged 0.1ppt higher
to 3.3% at the three-year horizon, though they were stable at
3.0% at the five-year horizon.
Local Data
The Westpac Economics New Zealand team published its
September Retail Spending Pulse , which found that retail
spending remained resilient in the face of hgher fuel prices
(see here).

Editorial 
Opportunities from the bond sell-off  
Strategy summary: The global bond sell-off is creating tactical 
and medium-term opportunities. In the US, September’s yield 
surge has improved the risk -reward in 10 -year government 
bonds, presenting an attractive entry point for investors with a 
6-12-month horizon. We believe bond and money markets are 
overly hawkish about the Fed outlook, given disinflation is likely 
to return as impact of oil prices and tariffs ease next year. 
In equities, we stick to quality, especially sectors riding the AI -
led earnings boom. The upcoming Q3 earnings season is likely 
to show AI -investments are powering sectors beyond the 
technology and communications services, such as financials, 
materials and power and electrification. We remain constructive 
on the semiconductor and memory segments. We also expect 
the US Dollar to peak in the next few weeks along with short -
to-medium-term US bond yields. This creates opportunities for 
AUD corporate bonds, Emerging Market USD bonds and gold. 
Attractive risk -reward in US 10 -year government bonds.  
The US 10-year bond yield rose more than 50bps in September 
(biggest monthly rise since 2022) and 115bps this year, amid 
expectations of economic resilience, higher Fed rates, fuel -
driven inflation, AI-related capital demand and fiscal concerns. 
Valuations appear close to fair value, improving the 6-12-month 
risk-reward. Bond arithmetic suggests losses of less than 1% if 
the yield rises to 6%, vs. more than 10% gains if it falls to 4.5%. 
Resilient US economic activity, but softer -than-expected 
inflation. September data showed robust US employment 
(jobless claims, ADP) and core capital-goods orders. However, 
consumer confidence remains depressed, real disposable 
income is flat and households are drawing on savings to sustain 
spending. Against this divided backdrop, core PCE inflation - 
the Fed’s preferred gauge - was softer than expected at 0.2% 
m/m and 3.0% y/y. A revised method to measure PCE inflation 
also indicate that annual core PCE inflation was 35bps lower 
than previously reported, narrowing the gap with CPI inflation. 
Markets appear overly hawkish on the Fed. Money markets 
price about 85bps of tightening over the next 12 months. A 
strong labour market (September’s non -farm payrolls data is 
due tonight) may justify one or two further hikes, but aggressive 
tightening after the sharp rise in bond yields – which has 
already tightened financial conditions - could threaten financial 
stability. US small- and mid-cap equities have already fallen 8–
9% from recent peaks over the past couple of months, 
materially underperforming large caps, as energy and 
borrowing-cost concerns intensify. W hile some Fed speakers 
remain hawkish, we  agree with New York Fed President 
Williams’ that there is no urgency to tighten. We see little 
chance of an October Fed rate hike and expect inflation data 
for Sep-Nov determining whether the Fed moves in December. 
Stay with quality equities and AI beneficiaries.  US large 
caps have absorbed the rise in bond yields relatively well, 
supported by expectations of robust earnings growth led by AI-
related investment. We continue to favour quality companies 
and sectors benefiting from this AI cycle. Semiconductors and 
memory remain preferred exposures: structural AI demand 
should extend the memory upcycle, while long -term supply 
agreements provide better revenue visibility. The opportunity is 
also broadening beyond technology and communication 
services into financials, materials, power and electrification. 
Opportunities in AUD corporate bonds and gold.  The USD 
rebound alongside higher US yields offers a tactical opportunity 
to add higher -yielding Emerging Market USD and non -USD 
bonds. We remain positive on AUD corporate bonds, which 
offer a yield premium over comparable US credit. Our 
constructive medium-term AUD/USD view could add returns for 
USD-based investors over the next 6 –12 months. We would 
also use gold’s recent pullback to add exposure, supported by 
sustained Emerging Market central bank demand. We continue 
to view USD 4,000/oz as important technical support for gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Top client questions 
  Do you expect more RBA rate hikes after its less hawkish 
guidance? What is your view on the AUD and AUD bonds? 
Our view: We expect the RBA to leave rates unchanged, but 
retain the option to hike again should inflation intensify. We 
maintain our medium-term constructive AUD view and maintain 
our opportunistically bullish AUD corporate bonds idea. 
Rationale: The RBA’s forward guidance remains data dependent, 
explicitly allowing further tightening if needed as inflation remains too 
high.  At 4.60%, Australia’s cash rate is currently 60–85bp above the 
Fed’s 3.75–4.00% range. Thus, even with our two additional 25bp s 
Fed hikes expectation, Australian rates would hold a modest positive 
rate differential. We expect the AUD to strengthen once the Fed 
signals that US rates are near their peak, which should allow resilient 
Australian fundamentals to reassert themselves. Key risks are 
persistently high US yields, weaker China growth or a sharper 
deterioration in global risk sentiment. 
We believe risk-reward has become more attractive for AUD bonds 
following the bear -flattening of the AUD yield curve. Markets are 
already pricing in one more RBA hike through end-2027, presenting 
an attractive opportunity to earn a yield even as increasingly 
restrictive financial conditions reinforc e disinflation over time. 
Meanwhile, the positive interest rate differential with the US supports 
the currency outlook, supporting the case for AUD bonds. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  What does the incoming US data tell us about the path for 
US 10-year bond yields from here? 
Our view: The risk -reward in US 10 -year yields has turned 
attractive following their move higher amid incoming US data. 
Rationale: Recent US data has indeed started to show softer-than-
expected inflation. However, market technicals are likely temporarily 
delaying a move lower in bond yields, with reports suggesting the  
rise in yields is creating forced selling amongst hedge funds and real 
estate investment trusts. Nevertheless, we do not expect this to be 
an extended selloff. We have initiated an opportunistic bullish idea 
on US 10-year government bonds this week and continue to believe 
this idea offers an attractive risk/reward on a 6-12-month horizon as 
yields become attractive enough to entice buyer flow to return. On a 
broader allocation basis, we retain our structural preference for 3-7-
year maturities in bonds. 
— Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

We remain positive on AUD corporate bonds amid 
Australia-US interest rate differentials and positive 
outlook for the AUD

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 2 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  How should investors position in the GBP ahead of the UK 
budget announcement next month? 
Our view: We maintain a medium-term constructive GBP view, 
but keep near-term exposure measured ahead of the 28 October 
Budget. We would view any pre -Budget weakness as an 
opportunity to gradually build GBP exposure. 
Rationale: The government’s decision to adjust the pension triple 
lock from 2030 is an early indication of greater willingness to tackle 
longer-term spending pressures, reinforcing Chancellor Healey’s 
commitment to fiscal discipline and meeting the fiscal rules. Markets 
are likely to scrutinise the Budget closely for evidence that this 
discipline extends to near -term tax and spending decisions. A 
credible Budget should help contain pressure on UK government 
bond yields and support GBP. Any fiscal slippage remains a key risk.  
Near term, we prefer measured GBP exposure as elevated US yields 
and repricing towards further Fed tightening could continue to 
support the USD. Over the medium term, we expect GBP to rebound 
as the Fed eventually signals the peak of its tightening cycle. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  How would different US mid-term election outcome 
scenarios affect the outlook for US equities? 
Our view: We believe election outcomes will have a limited 
impact over the next 12 months. We remain positive on US 
equities driven by fundamental earnings growth.  
Rationale: Over the past 50 years, the ruling US President’s party 
has retained control of both chambers of Congress on only two 
occasions (1978 and 2002). All other elections result ed in an 
opposition sweep or a divided government.  
Based on the latest prediction markets, it appears that an opposition 
sweep (both the House and Senate flips from Republican to 
Democrats) is more likely than a divided government ( in which the 
House flips to Democrats , while the Senate remains under 
Republican control). A scenario where Republicans retain a majority 
is least likely per prediction markets. 
While mid -term elections may introduce near -term volatility, we 
caution against overreaction. Historically, the average return for the 
S&P 500 following an opposition sweep is 17% over the next 12 
months, but the average return following a divided government is not 
significantly worse, at 15%. We prefer to base our investment views 
on fundamental earnings growth, where investor attention should 
focus on the upcoming Q3 earnings season. 
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is the outlook for  the memory chip and broader 
semiconductors industry? 
Our view: We remain positive on semiconductors and memory 
chips. Structural AI demand should prolong the memory 
upcycle while long-term agreements improve revenue visibility. 
Rationale: Recent industry commentary points to tighter memory 
supply-demand conditions through 2028, reinforcing our view that AI 
demand is likely to extend the memory upcycle. Furthermore, adoption 
of long-term agreements improves revenue and earnings visibility. 
Despite debates around “Pacing the Frontier”, we have seen a flurry 
of new model releases, along with the introduction of new consumer 
AI agents capable of comparing prices online and making 
purchases. These are early days, but the path is open for a business-
to-agent (B2A) economy where businesses increasingly interact with 
AI assistants acting on customers’ behalf. As these applications are 
increasingly inference intensive, wider adoption should support 
demand for memory, with its share of global semiconductor 
revenues projected to rise from 30.7% in 2025 to 66.7% in 2027. 
Against this backdrop, we maintain our Opportunistic idea on Global 
Semiconductors. Given memory  chips’ cyclical nature, investors 
should avoid overconcentration in memory names and maintain a 
diversified exposure across Big Tech and semiconductors. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Does a US bond yield stress test raise the risk to your 
constructive view on US equities? 
Our view: We have a target for the S&P 500 index of 8400 over 
the next 12 months. A scenario of a sustained rise in the US 10-
year government bond yield to 5.6% could lead to negative 
returns from the current S&P500 level (7,666). 
Rationale: Our base case 12-month target for the S&P 500 is 8,400, 
which assumes 2027 S&P 500 EPS of USD 420 and a 20x forward 
P/E multiple. A rise in bond yields would have an impact on equity 
valuations, as a higher discount rate reduces the present value of 
future earnings. W e stress -test our target under different 10 -year 
yield and earnings scenarios.  
A rise in bond yields would arguably be associated with a stronger 
growth environment and hence, even higher earnings. However, 
assuming unchanged EPS at our base case of USD 420, a 
(sustained) rise in the 10 -year bond yield to 5.6% would lead to 
negative returns from the current level  if not offset by a further 
improvement in earnings growth . However, in o ur base case  we 
expect the 10-year yield is more likely to fall from here. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

## Document 332: Spain’s rental dilemma_ protecting tenants won’t fix the housing shortage

Source PDF: all report pdf/Spain’s rental dilemma_ protecting tenants won’t fix the housing shortage.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

26
26
Real Estate
Rents in Spain
What do high-frequency
data tell us about rents
in Spain?
This article analyses the recent dynamics in Spain’s rental market based
on internal CaixaBank data on direct debit payments for residential rents.
According to our indicators, annual rent price increases are moderate
when there is no change of tenant or landlord, generally lying below 3%
and close to inflation, and consistent with regulatory caps. In contrast,

the pressure on the price of new rental contracts has increased, closing
2025 with growth in excess of 10% year-on-year. Despite this, the median
cost burden remains stable at around 30%, although there is still
significant inequality between age groups and greater pressure in large
cities.
In the last two decades, the proportion of the population that rents their dwelling 6 has
increased significantly in Spain. Whereas in 2007 only 19.4% of the population lived in a

home that was not their own, by 2024 this figure had risen to 26.3%. This change implies a
partial convergence towards the levels observed in the EU, where in 2024, 31.6% of the
population lived in rented accommodation.
In this article, we analyse the dynamics of rents in Spain using internal CaixaBank data on
rent payments, duly anonymised and aggregated. We analyse the payer-issuer relationships
by distinguishing between new rental contracts and existing contracts with rental price

updates, allowing trend shifts to be captured more precisely. Using this information, we
construct rental price indicators. This approach allows us to complement traditional data
sources with high-frequency and granular measures.
6  A tenant is defined
as anyone who does not
live in a property which
they own.

Rents for repeated leases have grown at close to inflation7
When analysing the year-on-year change in repeated rents – those where the contractual

relationship remains constant (same tenant and landlord) – moderate increases are
observed, generally close to inflation.
• The median growth of updated rents remains below 3% in all months between January
2018 and December 2025, reflecting the effect of regulatory limits applied during different
periods. During the freeze on rent increases in force during the pandemic, the median
was 0%. More recently, it has stabilised at around 2.3%, in line with the current limit for

contracts updated according to the housing rent update reference index (IRAV).8
• In parallel, the 75th percentile is around 2.9%, consistent with the maximum caps
applicable to contracts linked to the CPI.
• In contrast, the 90th percentile shows significantly higher increases, suggesting the
presence of specific situations, such as contract renewals or improvement works on flats,
where higher increases can occur.
7  This analysis focuses

debit, it does not include
those paid by transfer or
other payment methods.
As a result, the sample
may be partially biased
towards contracts
managed by real estate
companies rather than
individual landlords.
8  The housing rent
update index (IRAV)
establishes a formula
which sets the upper
limit for annual updates
to housing lease
contracts for rental
agreements signed from
26 May 2023 onwards.
For contracts signed
prior to this date, the
price adjustment clause

agreed in the contract
applies, generally linked
to the CPI.

The pressure on the price of new rentals is increasing
The analysis compares the price of new rental contracts9 in a given month with that of new
contracts signed in the same month of the previous year.10 Note that, by definition, this does
not compare the same rental contracts, as in the case of repeated rents; rather, it analyzes

the evolution of the price of the set of new leases signed in the reference month.
Following episodes of negative growth at the end of 2019 and at the start of the pandemic,
prices of newly signed rental contracts have picked up sharply. From 2024, the increases are
clearly above inflation, and in the final months of 2025 both the average and the median
year-on-year growth rates have exceeded 10%.

The median rental cost burden remains stable11
A crucial aspect to consider in the rental market analysis is the cost burden, which measures
the percentage of household income12 that is dedicated to paying rent. This metric shows
considerable stability.
• The median of the set of contracts is around 30% for the entire sample.
• However, in approximately 30% of contracts, the burden surpasses 40% (a threshold
widely considered excessive), and this proportion changes very little over time.

One factor that may contribute to the stability we observe in the rental cost burden is
residential mobility. When faced with contract renewals, some tenants move to a different
property in order to maintain a similar level of expenditure, even if it means adjusting to a
property with different characteristics (in terms of size, location or quality).
11  The cost burden
indicators have been
validated with other public
information sources.
Nevertheless, they should

be interpreted with
caution, especially the
values in the tails of the
distribution, due to
potential measurement
issues – e.g. it is possible
that the total income of
households operating with
more than one bank may
not be observed, or that
there could be cases such
as shared rentals that may
cause distortions.
12  Income includes
wages, unemployment
benefits, pensions or
other professional
incomes of the members
of each household. See
the CaixaBank Research

The rental cost burden is uneven across age groups
When breaking down the rental cost burden by age, we see a marked heterogeneity and
differentiated dynamics can be observed depending on the point in the life cycle.
• Young people and seniors exhibit the highest median levels of cost burden. However,
between 2020 and 2025 the burden among seniors decreases by 1.4 pps, while among
young people it increases by 1.1 pp in the same period.

• The increase in the rent burden for the young population is particularly significant, since
housing tenure is closely linked to the stage of the life cycle. Younger people tend to rent
(or live in rent-free housing), whereas, as age increases, the proportion of homeowners
rises sharply, reaching close to 90% among people over the age of 65.
• In contrast, middle-aged cohorts show more restrained cost burdens, generally between
20% and 25%. Even so, the 30 to 45 age group accounts for the largest cumulative increase:

+2.7 pps since 2018.

The rental cost burden is greater in large municipalities
The rental cost burden shows significant geographical differences, both among the set of
ongoing contracts and in the case of recently signed contracts.
• In large cities, defined in the sample as municipalities with more than 300,000 inhabitants,
the median burden is around 35%, and for new contracts it reaches between 35% and
40%, close to the threshold of excessive burden.

• In smaller municipalities, the burden is lower, generally between 25% and 30%. Even so,
in these areas as well, new rentals require a greater effort than existing contracts and
stand at around 30%.

In conclusion, although the median rent burden remains stable, the rental cost burden is

high for young and older people in new contracts and, especially, in large cities. These
results highlight the need to expand the housing supply in Spain and to do so in a way that
focuses on improving affordability in the most strained segments. An increase in supply
with this perspective would help alleviate rental market bottlenecks and reduce their impact
on key life cycle decisions – such as education, labour mobility or starting a family –
particularly among the most affected groups.

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research 
– 
2
8 
September 
2026

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.4% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4,25% - 4,50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth slowed to 4.3% y/y in Q2 2026 vs. 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth 
remains characterized by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports and the fragility of sectors that rely 
on domestic demand  has even widened this year . Domestic demand has weakened significantly in recent months . Retail sales have barely 
increased, the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting 
economic growth are expected to increase in the second half of the year. CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.0% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 2.8% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

ECONOMIC SCENARIO AND FORECAST

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.4% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would ease slightly to 1.2% in 2026 (1.3% in 2025), before edging back to 1.3% in 2027. The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averaging 3.2% in both 2026 and 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting second-
round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. The BoE 
is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

### Page 2

The Week Ahead October 2, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         2

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

India: RBI monetary policy committee 
meeting (7-Oct)

The macro environment has undergone 
significant changes since the RBI 
monetary policy committee’s (MPC) 
August policy meeting, with global oil 
prices rising past $100bl, and US Fed 
kickstarting their rate hike cycle. 
Domestically, core and headline 
continued to rise, laying the ground for 
inflation to quicken beyond 5.8 -6.0% in 
the second half as price risks broaden. 
Strong 1QFY27 GDP growth meanwhile 
pointed to the economy’s relative 
resilience to external shocks.

Besides fuel feeding into food costs, 
weather has also proved to be disruptive, 
with indications that the El Niño 
phenomenon could extend into 2027. 
International food benchmarks, including 
the UN Food Price index and the 
Bloomberg spot agricultural indice s, 
continue to climb. India’s food inflation 
has become more broad -based, shifting 
beyond vegetables and pulses to sugar, 
milk, protein groups, and edible oils. Sub-
par weather has contributed to this 
upmove, besides idiosyncratic factors 
like swing in production trends (cobweb), 
diversion for fuel usage etc.

After two years of normal monsoon, 
cumulative southwest rainfall this year is 
set to end with rainfall around 12 -13% 
below the long -term average, the most 
since 2015 and resulting in a slower 
build-up in reservoir levels.  
Global benchmark oil price gauge has 
averaged ~$93pb in FY27 year -to-date, 
up 36% yoy  vs FY26. Retail pump fuel 
prices were adjusted in quick succession 
in May26 and have been left unchanged 
since then, while other fuel products 
including CNG, aviation fuel, LPG etc 
have continued to climb. Add to this, 
other pockets like telecom, restaura nts, 
business services, transport fares etc 
have been inching up, reflecting early 
signs of broadening in price pressures (on 
sequential basis). A gradual broadening 
of price pressures is likely to keep 
headline inflation high and narrow the 
real rate buffer significantly.

Authorities are also conducting liquidity 
absorption measures through market 
operations like VRRR, open market 
operations and sell-buy FX swaps. This is 
likely to align the overnight call money 
rates to the benchmark repo rate, after 
having fallen sharply d ue to the surplus 
in the banking system.  Liquidity and core 
inflation have historically displayed a 
modest positive relationship, as per our 
study. As such, the RBI's inflation 
mandate has likely reinforced the push to 
drain excess liquidity.

We expect the RBI MPC to raise the repo 
rate by 25bp to 5.5% , citing pipeline 
inflationary pressures. Stance could be 
changed to withdrawal of 
accommodation to signal hawkish intent. 
We expect one more hike within 4Q26, 
though the odds of additional hikes have 
risen as oil prices stay stubbornly high 
and weather remains unfavourable.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead October 2, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Malaysia

We expect industrial production to grow 
by 5.8% yoy in August from 4.7% yoy in 
July. Strength in  export-oriented 
manufacturing is likely to persist , 
supported by sustained demand for 
artificial intelligence-related electronics 
goods, while mining output may have 
recovered from its contraction in July.

Taiwan

September trade and inflation data are 
forthcoming. Export growth is expected 
to sustain the 40% pace seen in the 
previous month. The global AI boom is 
facing rising headwinds from 
synchronised rate hikes by G3 central 
banks, higher bond yields, and rising  
financing costs. That said, there are so 
far no clear signs of a significant 
slowdown, based on the high -frequency 
indicators we track across AI demand, 
investment, and supply.

CPI is expected to rebound to 2.4% YoY 
in September, up from 2.0% in August. 
The earlier arrival of the Mid -Autumn 
Festival this year may have boosted food 
and services prices on a YoY basis. The 
rebound in global energy prices should 
also have lifted tran sport costs, 
including gasoline prices and airfares. 
While electricity tariffs will remain 
frozen, the December review implies a 
possibility of an electricity price hike 
after the local elections.

Given still -strong economic growth, 
persistent inflation, and the potential for 
inflation expectations to rise, we 
maintain our view that the central bank 
will raise the policy rate by a modest 
12.5bps to 2.125% in December.

Thailand

We expect Thailand’s headline inflation 
to accelerate to 3.2% yoy in September 
from 2.5% yoy  in August, slightly 
exceeding the central bank’s 1 -3% 
inflation target. Faster increases in 
energy and food prices likely pushed 
overall inflation higher, despite an 
easing in electricity tariffs starting 
September. We expect the Bank of 
Thailand (BOT), w hich views the rise in 
inflation as largely supply -driven, to 
keep its policy rate stable at 1.00% for 
the remainder of 2026, aiming to support 
an economy with low and uneven growth.

### Page 3

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Diplomacy appears firmly back in play. The past month saw continued fighting and renewed diplomacy.
Reporting suggests that the US is prepared to extend troop deployments in the Middle East to 2027. Iran
targeted both US forces in the region and some tankers moving through the Strait, but has been unable to
prevent a significant rebound in crude flows through Hormuz. On 22 SEP, talks were mediated by Qatar,

Pakistan and Egypt, and on 24 SEP, Iran presented the US with a seven-day roadmap to a ceasefire leading
to a phased reopening of the Strait of Hormuz and a return to nuclear negotiations, conditional on a US
lifting of its blockade of Iran, sanctions relief and broader cessation of hostilities. The Trump
Administration rejected the proposal, but negotiations continue.
To watch will be whether October is the month the war transitions into a negotiated settlement, which

may hinge on whether Iran is willing to make some concessions on sanctions relief and the nuclear
question, and whether Trump is keen to demonstrate that the war has produced a political and economic
result in the final weeks before the 3 NOV midterm elections. It is plausible that the current diplomatic
channel could produce a temporary de-escalation mechanism as long as there is pathway around the
broader nuclear issue.
Both sides will reconvene for trade talks in Beijing on 8-9 October, which the EU views as a deadline for

“tangible results”. Brussels remains focused on its rising trade deficit with China – which has reached a
record €1 billion per day – and the impact of Chinese goods on European industry. The bloc has vowed to
take measures if talks fail.
While both sides could achieve progress in talks, a lasting breakthrough appears improbable, setting the
stage for more trade tensions into 2027. New EU trade investigations, curbs on Chinese imports, and EU-
level instruments intended to address alleged Chinese overcapacity, are possible outcomes, to which

Beijing has stated it would respond.
China-EU trade talks could also impact China-US relations. Should talks with Brussels fall short of averting
increased European trade pressure, we assess that Beijing may further prioritise near-term US stability. See
Rules of Engagement: Mapping the Next Phase of China-US Ties for more.

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026

Latvia will elect all 100 members of the 15th Saeima. Latvia, which borders Russia and Belarus, has been
among the most vocal supporters of Ukraine. The next government will set the country’s course on
defence spending, Ukraine aid, sanctions enforcement and border security. The field is highly fragmented
with the governing parties lacking a majority in the polls. The fragmentation matters as a weakened and

multi-party scramble could mean a slower, more unstable coalition.
Brazil’s first electoral round is on 4 OCT, with a runoff on 25 OCT. This is a critical election as it will
decide whether Latin America’s biggest economy continues President Lula’s state-led social spending or
pivots to a more fiscally-orthodox agenda under Bolsonaro. Polls have gone from a Lula lead in the spring
to a statistical dead heat between Lula and Bolsonaro. A Brazilian Supreme Court crisis – which has

implicated Supreme Court Justices and politicians on both sides – is weighing on the race. Congressional
and gubernatorial races will also impact the governability of the country.
This election will also shape whether the country hedges between Washington and Beijing under Lula or
tilts toward closer US trade and security alignment under Bolsonaro with potential implications for
tariffs, BRICS+ cohesion, and Latin America’s broader positioning between the two powers.

The meetings will take place in Bangkok, bringing finance ministers, central bankers, policy-makers and
business leaders together. The agenda is expected to centre on global growth, financial stability, debt,
trade and investment, development finance and the impact of AI on jobs and productivity, with particular
attention given to mobilizing private capital. The key watchpoint will be the outlook for global growth
and inflation, debt vulnerabilities, the future of the international financial architecture and how policy-

makers respond to increasing fragmentation in trade and capital flows.

Leaders are expected to focus heavily on Ukraine, European security and defence, competitiveness and
the EU’s economic position in a more contested global environment. They will also take forward
negotiations on the 2028-2034 EU long-term budget. They set themselves a soft deadline in June to get
the budget broadly locked down because missing it pushes ratification into 2027 and risks a funding gap

for beneficiaries from JAN 2028. To watch will be a genuine breakthrough on the multiannual framework
or more likely what “substantial” progress may look like. Ukraine funding is the other pressure point. This
meeting will be important for understanding the direction of European defence spending and industrial
policy, the next phase of budget negotiations and the EU response to growing external economic
pressure and strategic dependencies.
The Canadian province of Alberta will hold a referendum, which includes a question about the province’s

future within Canada. This is not an independence vote itself but asking voters to choose between
remaining a Canadian province and asking the Alberta government to begin the constitutional process
required for a future binding referendum on separation. Current polling suggests “remain” is comfortably
ahead. The significance is around potential renewed uncertainty around Canada’s federal cohesion,
energy policy, fiscal transfers and Alberta's relationship with Ottawa and Washington. Even a modest

agreement to begin a process towards secession would likely keep alive equalization grievances as a
structural fault line in Canadian federalism feeding into political risk.
.
The event’s theme focuses on “Advancing Alliances in a New Era.” It will tackle how governments and
businesses respond to a world where geopolitics, economic security, supply chains and technology are
increasingly intertwined. There is likely to be a strong focus on the future of the transatlantic

relationship, including how Europe adapts to a more demanding security environment. There will also be
discussions of tariffs, supply chain resilience, critical capabilities and the weaponization of economic
interdependence. It will align with Chancellor Merz’s efforts to ensure Europe actively shapes outcomes
around strategic autonomy and reducing China exposure in manufacturing.

The second summit will take place in Saudi Arabi, bringing EU and Gulf leaders together. The agenda is

changes and personnel reshuffles, institutional reforms, and political priorities for the next Congress.

upcoming session to focus on personnel and governance matters.

Elections will be held for the 26th Knesset, the first since the October 7th attacks and wars in Gaza,

in Gaza factor into campaign messaging, and whether a Netanyahu-led bloc can reassemble a working
majority or whether the Bennett/Eisenkot-style “anyone but Bibi” coalitions gain enough traction. The

outcome will shape Israel’s posture on critical regional issues, including Gaza reconstruction, Iran policy
and West Bank settlement activity.
This summit is shaping up as a particularly important juncture against a more ambitious push to redefine
the relationship. During Mark Carney’s SEP visit to Strasbourg, he called for a comprehensive strategic
alliance with Europe. European Commission President von der Leyen went further, proposing that Canada

become the EU’s first “associate member”.
The agenda is expected to centre on trade and economic security, defense, critical minerals, energy,
digital cooperation and supply-chain resilience, building on the EU-Canada security and Defense
Partnership in the EU’s SAFE defense initiative. The summit will be watched for concrete progress on a
digital trade agreement and broader economic cooperation.

## Document 442: ドル高・米金利上昇への転換で問われる新興国通貨の耐性

Source PDF: all report pdf/ドル高・米金利上昇への転換で問われる新興国通貨の耐性.pdf
Report source: DIR
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

新興国

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス　〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番 1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー

2026 年 10 月 5 日　全 9 頁

ドル高・米金利上昇への転換で問われる新
興国通貨の耐性 
耐性は、メキシコ、マレーシア、ブラジルで比較的高い

経済調査部 シニアエコノミスト 増川 智咲

[要約] 
 2026 年の新興国通貨は、中東危機を背景とする原油高とリスク回避姿勢の強まり、米
国の実質金利上昇に伴う対米金利差の縮小という共通のショックを受けたが、通貨間
のパフォーマンスには差があった。 
 2026年4～6月には、多くの国で交易条件や輸出入数量の動向、サービス・所得収支を
含む経常収支の構造が、通貨の強弱と概ね整合した。ただし、インド、南アフリカ
（南ア） 、トルコでは、経常収支だけでは為替動向を説明できず、資金フローやインフ
レ期待などの金融面の影響を大きく受けた。また、2026 年 7 月以降は、ドル安に加え、
各国の利上げや為替介入等が通貨を下支えしてきた。 
 9 月 16 日の米国の利上げと追加利上げ観測は、新興国通貨にとって再びリスクとなり
うる。今後の耐性を総合すると、メキシコ、マレーシア、ブラジルは、経常収支、外
貨獲得力、直接投資の規模、外貨準備の厚みなどの面で相対的に下支え材料が多い。
一方、インドネシアとフィリピンは厚い外貨準備を有するものの、経常赤字と市場性
資金の変動に注意が必要である。タイはエネルギー高に対して脆弱ではあるが、外貨
獲得力や直接投資、外貨準備の厚みが緩衝材になる見通しである。対象国の中では、
海外借入依存度の高さ、期待インフレの高さ、低い政策信認という点で、トルコの脆
弱性が最も大きい。

### Page 2

1

2 / 9

2026 年の新興国通貨：共通ショックの後に鮮明となった国別格差 
2026 年の新興国通貨を取り巻く主なリスクには、中東危機を背景とする原油高とリスク回避
姿勢の強まり、米国の実質金利上昇に伴う対米金利差の縮小、ドル高への転換などが挙げられ
る。ただし、各要因の強さは時期によって大きく変化した。このため、本稿では新興国通貨の
対ドル為替レートの動向を 3 つの局面に分けて整理する（図表 1） 。 
第 1 の局面は、中東危機が生じる前の①2025 年 12 月末～2026 年 2 月 28 日である。この時期
は、米連邦準備制度理事会（FRB）の独立性をめぐる懸念や利下げ期待などを背景にドル安が
進み、多くの新興国通貨が対ドルで上昇した。 
第2の局面は、②米国によるイラン攻撃から、4月8日の米国・イラン間の初回停戦発表まで
である。中東情勢の緊迫化に伴う原油価格の上昇と、金融市場におけるリスク回避姿勢の強ま
りを受け、新興国通貨は対ドルで幅広く下落した。 
第 3 の局面は、③初回停戦発表から FRB による利上げ直前（2026 年 9 月 11 日）までである。
ただし、この期間も中東情勢は完全には安定せず、為替市場を動かす要因は変化した。 
本稿では、第 3 局面を 4～6 月期の③-1 と、7 月から FRB 利上げ直前の 9 月 11 日までの③-2
とし、前者については貿易・経常収支、後者についてはドル相場、金利差、資本フロー、政策
対応を軸として検討する。 
図表 1 新興国通貨の対ドル為替レート変化率と米ドル実効レートの変化率（2026 年の期間別）

（注 2）米ドルは、プラスが実効レートの上昇を表す。

通貨間格差の要因①～貿易・経常収支を通じた影響 
まず、③-1 にあたる 2026 年 4～6 月期について、通貨パフォーマンスの差を貿易・経常収支
の面から確認する。図表2～4は、各国の経常収支と貿易収支の変動要因を示している。資源価
格の上昇などに伴う交易条件の変化だけでなく、輸出入数量、サービス収支、所得収支も併せ
て確認する。

経常収支悪化と通貨安が整合的だったタイ、インドネシア、フィリピン 
③-1 の期間に対ドルで為替レートが下落した国のうち、タイ、インドネシア、フィリピンで
は、2026 年に入って経常赤字（GDP 比）が拡大した（図表 2） 。経常赤字の拡大は、これらの通
貨が軟調に推移したことと整合的である。 
タイとフィリピンでは、貿易赤字の拡大が経常収支悪化の主因となった。背景には、エネル
ギー価格上昇による交易条件の悪化と、輸入数量の増加がある（図表 3、4） 。両国は、本稿の
対象国の中でもエネルギー輸入への依存度が高い。加えて、AI ブームを背景に電子機器などの
輸出向け生産が拡大し、中間財輸入が増加した可能性がある。ただし、輸出向け中間財の輸入
増は、将来の生産・輸出拡大に伴う側面もあるため、外部脆弱性の悪化と一概に評価できない
点には留意が必要である。 
インドネシアでは、原油高の局面においても貿易黒字を維持したが、黒字幅が縮小し、経常
赤字の拡大につながった。貿易黒字縮小の原因には、交易条件の悪化に加え、輸出数量の減少
があった。同国の輸出構造は資源の割合が比較的高く、マレーシアに比べて AI関連の電子機器
輸出拡大の恩恵を受けにくい。最大の輸出先である中国の内需減速も、輸出の下押し要因とな
ったとみられる。

経常収支だけでは通貨安を説明しにくいインドとトルコ 
経常収支の動向だけで通貨安を十分に説明できない国がインドとトルコである。インドはエ
ネルギーの純輸入国であり、2026 年 4～6 月期の財の貿易赤字の規模は GDP 比 10％近くにまで
拡大した。一方、IT・ビジネスサービスを中心とするサービス収支と、海外送金を中心とする
第二次所得収支の黒字が財の貿易赤字の相当分を相殺し、経常赤字は比較的小幅にとどまった。 
トルコもエネルギーの純輸入国であることから、2026年4～6月期は交易条件が悪化した。た
だし、高金利による需要抑制が消費財輸入を抑え、貿易赤字と経常赤字はいずれも前期比で縮
小した。それにもかかわらず、対象国の中でリラの下落率が最も大きかったことは、経常取引
以外の要因が強く作用していたことを示唆している。

### Page 4

1

4 / 9

経常取引が通貨の下支えとなったマレーシアとメキシコ 
マレーシアとメキシコでは、良好な経常取引が通貨の下支えとなったとみられる。両国は
2026年4～6月期に貿易黒字・経常黒字を維持した。マレーシアでは天然ガス価格の上昇に伴う
交易条件の改善に加え、半導体・電子機器などの輸出数量の増加が寄与した。同国の貿易黒字
は GDP 比 7.5％に達し、経常黒字を支えた。 
メキシコでは、米国向けを中心とする工業品輸出の増加が貿易黒字に大きく寄与した。ニア
ショアリングを背景とする生産拠点の集積も、輸出基盤を支えているとみられる。両国では、
堅調な財の輸出を背景とした外貨獲得力が、通貨の下支え材料となった。

経常赤字の「質」が異なるブラジルと、経常取引だけで通貨高を説明できない南ア 
ブラジルでは、資源価格の上昇に伴う交易条件の改善により、2026年4～6月期の貿易黒字幅
は前期から拡大した。一方、外国企業への配当支払いや国債の利子払い等による第一次所得収
支の大幅な赤字を背景に、経常収支は赤字となった。ブラジルの経常赤字は、輸出競争力の低
下やエネルギー輸入の増加に起因するものではない。この点で、貿易赤字の拡大を伴う国と比
べて、対外収支面の脆弱性は相対的に小さい。 
南アフリカ（南ア）では、原油高による交易条件の悪化を受け、2026年4～6月期の貿易黒字
幅が前期から大きく縮小した。さらに、第一次所得収支の赤字規模が大きいことから、経常収
支は GDP比▲0.9％の赤字となった。経常収支が悪化した一方、ランドが対ドルで上昇した背景
には、資本フローなどの経常取引以外の要因が考えられる。 
このように、③-1 では、多くの国で、貿易・経常収支の動向が通貨パフォーマンスの差と一
定程度整合した。米国の金利が相対的に安定していて金利差による変動が目立ちにくかったこ
とに加え、エネルギー高や AIブームといった貿易・経常収支要因が主な為替変動要因だったた
めだと考えられる。 
例外は、インド、南ア、トルコである。 3 カ国では、経常収支と通貨パフォーマンスが必ず
しも一致しなかった。また 7月以降である③-2の期間になると、③-1に経常収支が悪化してい
たタイとインドネシアで為替レートが対ドルで上昇に転じた。つまり、③全体の通貨動向を説
明するには、経常取引だけでなく、金利差を意識した資本フローや政策対応など、金融面の要
因による影響が大きい可能性があるため、次節で考察する。

人投資家の証券売買や金利動向など、より高頻度の指標を用いる。 
トルコでは、2026 年 1～3 月期に資本流出圧力が強まった後、4～6 月期には証券投資やその
他投資が大幅流入超となり、中央銀行も外貨準備を積み増すなど、国際収支面では改善がみら
れた（図表 5） 。8 月の外国人売買統計でも、株式市場へは 6.5 億ドルの流入超となったことが
わかる。このように、資本フローを引き付けているにもかかわらず、リラ安が進んだ背景には、
市場参加者のインフレ期待や、将来的な通貨下落予想、政策運営への信認欠如といった、フロ
ー統計に表れにくい要因があったとみられる。また、4～6 月期に流入した資本の大半は、証券
投資やその他投資で、直接投資は流出超であった。安定的な長期資金よりも市場環境に左右さ
れやすい資金への依存は、リラ相場の不安定性を高める要因となりうる。 
インドでは2025年10～12月期以降、3四半期連続で証券投資とデリバティブを中心に流出超
となった。特に、対内証券投資（ネット）のマイナスが大きかった。それまで投資を積み増し
ていた海外投資家によるポジション調整や、対米金利差の縮小などが資金流出につながった可
能性がある。ルピー安には、経常取引よりも金融要因が強く作用した可能性が高い。 
南アは、4～6 月期に証券投資が大きく流出超となった。外国人売買統計を見ると、株式市場
からの資本流出が顕著だった。他方、比較的高い金利水準を背景に、債券市場には 4 月以降連
続して資金が流入した。外国人売買統計では、8 月に債券と株式の合計（ネット）が 85 億ラン
ドと 4 カ月ぶりのプラスとなった。ランドの上昇には、経常取引要因以上に、金利差を意識し
た債券市場への資金流入が寄与したとみられる。

2026 年 7 月からFRB 利上げ直前までの期間（③-2）に、通貨が上昇に転じた国 
③-2では、③-1に軟調だったタイバーツとインドネシアルピアが対ドルで上昇に転じ、マレ
ーシアリンギットも上昇した。短期間で経常収支の構造が大きく変化したとは考えにくいこと
から、3 通貨の持ち直しには、金融面の要因が強く作用したとみられる。 
マレーシアでは、4～6 月期の経常黒字と輸出基盤が対外収支の下支えとなる中、7 月以降の
ドル安がリンギット上昇に寄与した。非居住者による国内債券保有は 7 月に減少したものの、
8 月には増加に転じており、期間後半には債券フローもリンギットの下支え要因となった。 
タイバーツの反転には、広範なドル安が最も直接的に作用したとみられる。これに加え、 7
月には金・燃料輸入の減少を背景に経常赤字が前月から縮小したほか、電子関連輸出や観光に
も持ち直しがみられ、対外収支と景気への警戒が和らいだ可能性がある。 
インドネシアでは、2026年5・6月に累計100bpの利上げが実施されたほか、NDF・スポット・
DNDF1を通じた為替安定化策がルピアを下支えした。7 月以降は外国人投資家による国内金融資

1 NDF は、取引対象通貨の現物受け渡しを行わず、事前に定めた契約レートと決済時点の実勢レートとの差額
を、通常は米ドルで差金決済する取引。DNDF は、国内市場において、取引対象通貨の現物受渡しを行わず、事
前に定めた契約レートと決済時点の実勢レートとの差額を現地通貨で差金決済する取引。

産への資金フローにも改善がみられ、ルピアの持ち直しを下支えしたとみられる。

各国の対外資金調達構造：トルコの脆弱性が対象国の中で相対的に大きい 
以下では、対外債務の規模と通貨構成、対外負債の構成、ネットの対外資産・負債、外貨準
備と国内金融市場の厚み、の 4 つの観点から各国の耐性を評価する（図表 6、7） 。 
対象国の中で、ドル高局面への脆弱性が最も大きいと考えられるのはトルコである。対外債
務の規模が比較的大きく、その多くが外貨建てである。対外負債に占める銀行借入などの「そ
の他投資」の割合も58％（2025年）と高く、リスク回避局面では借換え条件の悪化や資本流出
圧力が強まりやすい（図表 7） 。 
トルコ以外の対象国では、脆弱性は相対的に限定される。マレーシアは対外債務残高の規模
が大きいものの、対外資産も厚く、対外純資産残高は GDP比▲5.5％、つまり小幅な対外純負債
にとどまる。また、対外負債の約 50％を直接投資が占め、負債構造は比較的安定している。対
外負債に占める直接投資の割合という観点では、ブラジルとタイでも高く、短期の市場性資金
への依存を抑える要因となっている。 
南アは、対外資産が対外負債を上回る純債権国であり、対外債務に占める外貨建ての割合も
低い。国内投資家層が比較的厚く、自国通貨建てで資金を調達しやすいことが、為替ミスマッ
チを抑制している。 
インドネシア、フィリピンでは、対外債務に占める外貨建ての割合が高い。ただし、債務の
長期化や為替ヘッジなどにより、ドル高による短期的な返済負担の増加は一定程度抑制されて

いるとみられる。もっとも、インドネシアやフィリピンでは、対外負債に占める証券投資の割
合が比較的大きく、リスク回避局面では短期的な資本流出が生じやすい。フィリピンは国内投

応の外貨準備を有し、地域金融セーフティーネットも存在するため、対外流動性リスクを緩和
する余地はある。ただし、これらは通貨下落を完全に防ぐものではなく、外部環境の悪化時に
は一時的に下落圧力が強まる可能性がある。

FRB 利上げ後の新興国通貨：相対的に有利なマレーシア、メキシコ、ブラジ
ル 
9 月 16 日の米国の利上げと追加利上げ観測は、新興国通貨にとって再びリスクとなりうるが、
その影響は一様ではないだろう。総合的に判断すると、メキシコ、マレーシア、ブラジルは相
対的に下支え材料が多い。ただし、その根拠は異なる。メキシコは工業品輸出と直接投資、マ
レーシアは経常黒字と対外資産、ブラジルは貿易黒字、直接投資、金利差（図表 8）が主な緩
衝材となる。一方、南アとインドは対外バランスシートやサービス輸出に強みを持つものの、
証券フローの変動には注意が必要である。 
インドネシアとフィリピンは、外貨準備の厚みという点でバッファーを有するが、経常赤字
や外国人投資家のリスク回避に対する通貨の感応度が高い。タイはエネルギー高に対して脆弱
ではあるが、半導体・電子機器輸出による外貨獲得能力や直接投資、外貨準備の厚みが緩衝材
になる見通しである。対象国の中で最も脆弱なのはトルコである。海外借入への依存度が高い
うえ、不安定なインフレ期待と政策への低い信認がリラの下落圧力を増幅しやすいためである。 
以上のとおり、外部環境の変化に対する新興国通貨への影響は、経常収支の黒字・赤字だけ
ではなく、外貨獲得力、資金調達方法、資本流入の安定性、外貨準備、対外債務の通貨・満期
構成、政策への信認に左右されそうである。
